The D ℏ D_{\hslash} -module mirror conjecture for flag varieties
Chi Hong Chow

Abstract
. Rietsch constructed a candidate T T -equivariant mirror LG model for any flag variety G / P G/P . In this paper, we prove the following mirror symmetry prediction: the small T × 𝔾 m T\times\mathbb{G}_{m} -equivariant quantum cohomology of G / P G/P equipped with quantum ℏ \hslash -connection is isomorphic as D ℏ D_{\hslash} -modules to the Brieskorn lattice associated to the LG model equipped with Gauss-Manin ℏ \hslash -connection.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
Primary 14J33; Secondary 14D24, 14M15, 14N35

1.
introduction

1.1.
Main result

Let
G
be a simple simply-connected complex algebraic group and
P\subseteq G
a parabolic subgroup. Motivated by the work of Batyrev, Ciocan-Fontanine, Kim and van Straten
[
1
]
, Givental
[
15
]
, Joe and Kim
[
23
]
and Peterson (unpublished, but see
[
43
]
), Rietsch
[
45
]
constructed a candidate
T
-equivariant mirror of
G/P
in terms of the Langlands dual group
G^{\vee}
. It is a quintuple
(X^{\vee}_{P},W,\pi,p,\omega_{X^{\vee}_{P}})
where

•

X^{\vee}_{P}
is a smooth affine variety;

•

W\in\mathcal{O}(X^{\vee}_{P})
is a regular function;

•

\pi:X^{\vee}_{P}\rightarrow Z(L^{\vee})
is a smooth morphism onto a subtorus
Z(L^{\vee})
of
T^{\vee}
;

•

p:X^{\vee}_{P}\rightarrow T^{\vee}
is a morphism; and

•

\omega_{X^{\vee}_{P}}\in\Omega^{top}(X^{\vee}_{P}/Z(L^{\vee}))
is a fiberwise volume form.

In this paper, we prove

Theorem 1.1
.

The
D_{\hslash}
-module mirror conjecture holds for the above mirror pair.

That is, we prove that some
D_{\hslash}
-modules associated to
G/P
and
(X^{\vee}_{P},W,\pi,p,\omega_{X^{\vee}_{P}})
are isomorphic.

On the A-side, we have an
H^{\bullet}_{T\times\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\pt)\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hslash]}D_{\hslash,(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{|I\setminus I_{P}|}}
-module
QH^{\bullet}_{T\times\mathbb{G}_{m}}(G/P)[q_{i}^{-1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]
, the
small
T\times\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant quantum cohomology of
G/P
, where the
D_{\hslash}
-module structure is given by the
quantum
\hslash
-connection

\nabla_{\partial_{q_{i}}}:=\hslash\partial_{q_{i}}+q_{i}^{-1}c_{1}^{T\times\mathbb{G}_{m}}(L_{\omega_{i}})\star-.

On the B-side, we have a
\sym^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})[\hslash]\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hslash]}D_{\hslash,Z(L^{\vee})}
-module

\displaystyle G_{0}(X^{\vee}_{P},W,\pi,p)

\displaystyle:=~

\displaystyle\coker\left(\sym^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})[\hslash]\otimes\Omega^{top-1}(X^{\vee}_{P}/Z(L^{\vee}))\xrightarrow{\partial}\sym^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})[\hslash]\otimes\Omega^{top}(X^{\vee}_{P}/Z(L^{\vee}))\right)

where

\partial(z\otimes\omega):=z\otimes(\hslash d\omega+dW\wedge\omega)-\sum_{i}zh_{i}\otimes(p^{*}\langle h^{i},\mccccc_{T^{\vee}}\rangle)\wedge\omega.

It is called the
Brieskorn lattice
and its
D_{\hslash,Z(L^{\vee})}
-module structure is given by the
Gauss-Manin
\hslash
-connection

\eta\cdot[z\otimes\omega]:=\left[z\otimes(\hslash\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{\eta}}\omega+(\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{\eta}}W)\omega)-\sum_{i}zh_{i}\otimes(\iota_{\widetilde{\eta}}p^{*}\langle h^{i},\mccccc_{T^{\vee}}\rangle)\omega\right].

In addition to the
D_{\hslash}
-module structures, both
QH_{T\times\mathbb{G}_{m}}^{\bullet}(G/P)[q_{i}^{-1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]
and
G_{0}(X^{\vee}_{P},W,\pi,p)
have two structures, namely the
grading
and the
shift operators
. At the semi-classical limit
\hslash\to 0
, these modules become the
T
-equivariant quantum cohomology ring

QH_{T}^{\bullet}(G/P)[q_{i}^{-1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]
and the
Jacobi ring

\jac(X^{\vee}_{P},W,\pi,p)
respectively.

Theorem 1.2
.

(Precise version of Theorem
1.1
) After identifying the base algebras via some canonical isomorphisms

\sym^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})[\hslash]\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{\bullet}_{T\times\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\pt)\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathcal{O}(Z(L^{\vee}))\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}[q_{i}^{\pm 1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}],

there exists a
\sym^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})[\hslash]\otimes\mathcal{O}(Z(L^{\vee}))
-linear map

\Phi_{mir}:G_{0}(X^{\vee}_{P},W,\pi,p)\rightarrow QH^{\bullet}_{T\times\mathbb{G}_{m}}(G/P)[q_{i}^{-1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]

satisfying

(1)

it is bijective;

(2)

it is
D_{\hslash,Z(L^{\vee})}
-linear;

(3)

\Phi_{mir}([\omega_{X^{\vee}_{P}}])=1
;

(4)

at the semi-classical limit, it becomes a ring isomorphism

\Phi_{mir}^{\hslash=0}:\jac(X^{\vee}_{P},W,\pi,p)\xrightarrow{\sim}QH_{T}^{\bullet}(G/P)[q_{i}^{-1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}];

(5)

it intertwines the shift operators; and

(6)

it is graded.

Any
\sym^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})[\hslash]\otimes\mathcal{O}(Z(L^{\vee}))
-linear map satisfying (3) and (5) must be equal to
\Phi_{mir}
.

Remark 1.3
.

Property (5) implies that for any character
\lambda
of
T^{\vee}
, the isomorphism
\Phi_{mir}^{\hslash=0}
from (4) sends
[\lambda\circ p]
to the
T
-equivariant Seidel’s element

[
51
]
associated to the cocharacter
\lambda:\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow T
.

1.2.
Outline of proof

We explain how to construct
\Phi_{mir}
.

Let us first explain how to construct the ring isomorphism
\Phi_{mir}^{\hslash=0}
based on known results from the literature. Introduce two rings:

(i)

\mathcal{O}(B_{e^{T}}^{\vee})
, the coordinate ring of the base change of the universal centralizer of
G^{\vee}
along the quotient morphism
\mathfrak{t}^{\vee}\rightarrow\mathfrak{t}^{\vee}/W\simeq\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}/\!\!/G^{\vee}
.

(ii)

H_{\bullet}^{T}(\mathcal{G}r)
, the
T
-equivariant Pontryagin homology of the affine Grassmannian
\mathcal{G}r
of
G
.

Recall the following results:

(1)

Rietsch
[
45
]
constructed a ring map

\widetilde{\Phi}_{R}:\mathcal{O}(B_{e^{T}}^{\vee})\rightarrow\jac(X^{\vee}_{P},W,\pi,p)

which induces an isomorphism

\Phi_{R}:\mathcal{O}(B_{e^{T}}^{\vee}\times_{G^{\vee}}U^{\vee}_{-}(\dot{w}_{P}\dot{w}_{0})^{-1}B^{\vee}_{-})\xrightarrow{\sim}\jac(X^{\vee}_{P},W,\pi,p).

(2)

Using the geometric Satake equivalence, Yun and Zhu
[
53
]
constructed a ring isomorphism

\Phi_{YZ}:\mathcal{O}(B_{e^{T}}^{\vee})\xrightarrow{\sim}H^{T}_{-\bullet}(\mathcal{G}r).

(3)

Discovered by Peterson
[
43
]
and proved by Lam and Shimozono
[
32
]
, there exists a ring map

\Phi_{PLS}:H^{T}_{-\bullet}(\mathcal{G}r)\rightarrow QH_{T}^{\bullet}(G/P)[q_{i}^{-1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]

which is surjective after localization and sends every affine Schubert class to either 0 or an explicit quantum Schubert class.

(4)

The author of the present paper
[
10
]
proved that the composition
\Phi_{PLS}\circ\Phi_{YZ}
descends to a ring isomorphism

\Phi_{P}:\mathcal{O}(B_{e^{T}}^{\vee}\times_{G^{\vee}}U^{\vee}_{-}(\dot{w}_{P}\dot{w}_{0})^{-1}B^{\vee}_{-})\xrightarrow{\sim}QH_{T}^{\bullet}(G/P)[q_{i}^{-1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}].

This gives the Peterson variety presentation.

To summarize, we have the following commutative diagram

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

The composition

\Phi_{mir}^{\hslash=0}:=\Phi_{P}\circ\Phi^{-1}_{R}

gives the desired ring isomorphism.

Now, to construct
\Phi_{mir}
, we “quantize” everything. More precisely, we replace the above rings (resp. ring maps) by
\mathbb{C}[\hslash]
-modules (resp.
\mathbb{C}[\hslash]
-linear maps) and establish the analogue of the commutative diagram (
1.1
) which recovers the original one by taking
-\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hslash]}\mathbb{C}
.

(
\hslash
i)

We replace
\mathcal{O}(B_{e^{T}}^{\vee})
by the desymmetrized quantum Toda lattice
\delta_{\hslash}(\mathcal{K})
of
G^{\vee}
in the sense of Bezrukavnikov and Finkelberg
[
6
]
.

(
\hslash
ii)

We replace
H_{\bullet}^{T}(\mathcal{G}r)
by the
T\times\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant homology
H_{\bullet}^{T\times\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\mathcal{G}r)
of
\mathcal{G}r
where
\mathbb{G}_{m}
corresponds to the loop rotation.

(
\hslash
1)

We construct

\widetilde{\Phi}_{R}^{\hslash}:\delta_{\hslash}(\mathcal{K})\rightarrow G_{0}(X^{\vee}_{P},W,\pi,p)

by hand. This map is somewhat canonical: for any
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\in\mathfrak{t}^{\vee}
and
f\in\mathcal{O}(G^{\vee})
satisfying
f(u\cdot-)=f(-)
whenever
u\in U^{\vee}_{-}
, we have

\widetilde{\Phi}_{R}^{\hslash}([x_{1}^{L}\cdots x_{n}^{L}f])=[(x_{1}+\hslash\rho^{\vee}(x_{1}))\cdots(x_{n}+\hslash\rho^{\vee}(x_{n}))\otimes f|_{X^{\vee}_{P}}\omega_{X^{\vee}_{P}}]

where
\rho^{\vee}
is the Weyl covector. We show that
\widetilde{\Phi}_{R}^{\hslash}
induces

\Phi_{R}^{\hslash}:\delta_{\hslash}(\mathcal{K})\otimes\mathcal{O}(Z(L^{\vee}))/\mathcal{W}\rightarrow G_{0}(X^{\vee}_{P},W,\pi,p)

for some submodule
\mathcal{W}
which will be defined in Section
7.3
. Unlike the semi-classical case, we are only able to show that
\Phi_{R}^{\hslash}
is surjective.

(
\hslash
2)

We take
\Phi_{YZ}^{\hslash}
to be the isomorphism

\Phi_{BF}^{\delta}:\delta_{\hslash}(\mathcal{K})\xrightarrow{\sim}H_{-\bullet}^{T\times\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\mathcal{G}r)

constructed by Bezrukavnikov and Finkelberg
[
6
]
.

(
\hslash
3)

We define

\Phi_{PLS}^{\hslash}:H_{-\bullet}^{T\times\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\mathcal{G}r)\rightarrow QH_{T\times\mathbb{G}_{m}}^{\bullet}(G/P)[q_{i}^{-1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]

to be the natural
T\times\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant lift of
\Phi_{PLS}
. (Recall
\Phi_{PLS}
sends affine Schubert classes to either 0 or quantum Schubert classes and these classes have natural
T\times\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant lifts.)

(
\hslash
4)

We show that
\Phi_{PLS}^{\hslash}\circ\Phi_{YZ}^{\hslash}
induces

\Phi^{\hslash}_{P}:\delta_{\hslash}(\mathcal{K})\otimes\mathcal{O}(Z(L^{\vee}))/\mathcal{W}\rightarrow QH_{T\times\mathbb{G}_{m}}^{\bullet}(G/P)[q_{i}^{-1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}].

Unlike the semi-classical case, we are only able to show that
\Phi_{P}^{\hslash}
is surjective.

Although we do not know whether
\Phi_{R}^{\hslash}
and
\Phi_{P}^{\hslash}
are bijective, we are able to show that there exists a unique map
\Phi_{mir}
which is bijective such that

\Phi_{mir}\circ\Phi_{R}^{\hslash}=\Phi_{P}^{\hslash}.

To conclude, we not only manage to construct
\Phi_{mir}
but also establish the analogue of the commutative diagram (
1.1
). For potential applications, let us also quote the latter result as a theorem. For simplicity, we drop
\delta_{\hslash}(\mathcal{K})\otimes\mathcal{O}(Z(L^{\vee}))/\mathcal{W}
from the diagram below.

Theorem 1.4
.

The mirror isomorphism
\Phi_{mir}
from Theorem
1.2
fits into the commutative diagram

\hbox to312.33pt{\vbox to110.25pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 37.8805pt\lower-94.01129pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{202.70232pt}{-46.22635pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{71}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}{}{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{}{}{}}{}{
{}{}{}}
{{{{{}}{
{}{}}{}{}{{}{}}}}}{}{{{{{}}{
{}{}}{}{}{{}{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}{}{}{{{}{}}}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 52.69 -118.11 L 170.6 -118.11}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{123.49307pt}{-85.35828pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 C -1.18 -0.39 -2.35 -1.33 -2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{70.51895pt}{-80.75133pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{71}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

In fact, as an
\mathcal{O}(Z(L^{\vee}))
-linear map,
\Phi_{mir}
is uniquely determined by this diagram.

Finally, let us explain briefly how to verify that
\Phi_{mir}
is
D_{\hslash,Z(L^{\vee})}
-linear. The
\mathcal{O}(Z(L^{\vee}))
-linearity follows immediately from the construction. It remains to show that
\Phi_{mir}
intertwines the operators
\partial_{q_{i}}\cdot-
arising from the source and target. According to Bezrukavnikov-Finkelberg’s theory
[
6
]
,
\delta_{\hslash}(\mathcal{K})
is obtained from the quantum Toda lattice
\kappa_{\hslash}(\mathcal{K})
by extension of scalars. The latter is a ring, and hence
\delta_{\hslash}(\mathcal{K})
is a right
\kappa_{\hslash}(\mathcal{K})
-module. Moreover,
\Phi_{BF}^{\delta}
is obtained from an isomorphism

\Phi_{BF}^{\kappa}:\kappa_{\hslash}(\mathcal{K})\xrightarrow{\sim}H_{-\bullet}^{G\times\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\mathcal{G}r)

of rings by extension of scalars, and hence
\Phi_{BF}^{\delta}
is a module isomorphism with respect to
\Phi_{BF}^{\kappa}
.

By a straightforward computation, each B-side operator
\partial_{q_{i}}\cdot-
can be lifted to an operator on
\delta_{\hslash}(\mathcal{K})\otimes\mathcal{O}(Z(L^{\vee}))
given by
\hslash\partial_{q_{i}}
plus the right multiplication by an element
x_{i}
of
\kappa_{\hslash}(\mathcal{K})\otimes\mathcal{O}(Z(L^{\vee}))
. We show that after extension of scalars,
\Phi_{PLS}^{\hslash}\circ\Phi_{BF}^{\kappa}
sends
x_{i}
to
q_{i}^{-1}c_{1}^{T\times\mathbb{G}_{m}}(L_{\omega_{i}})
, the zeroth order term of the A-side operator
\nabla_{\partial_{q_{i}}}
. By generalizing our recent new proof
[
9
]
of Peterson-Lam-Shimozono’s theorem
[
32
,
43
]
, we show that
\Phi_{PLS}^{\hslash}
is induced by the
H_{\bullet}^{G\times\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\mathcal{G}r)
-module action on
QH^{\bullet}_{G\times\mathbb{G}_{m}}(G/P)[q_{i}^{-1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]
(a.k.a. non-abelian shift operators) recently constructed by González, Mak and Pomerleano
[
17
]
. We conclude the proof by applying the facts that
\Phi_{BF}^{\delta}
is a module isomorphism with respect to
\Phi_{BF}^{\kappa}
and that non-abelian shift operators commute with
\nabla_{\partial_{q_{i}}}
.

1.3.
Related work

Mirror symmetry has been extensively researched and we are unable to review the literature. Let us mention however some works with which our results fit.

(1)

The
D_{\hslash}
-module mirror conjecture (DMC) for toric varieties has been proved in great generality. The first non-trivial case, which follows from Givental’s mirror theorem
[
16
]
, assumes the toric varieties to be smooth, projective and semi-Fano. The result has been extended numerous times. The most general case, proved by Coates, Corti, Iritani and Tseng
[
11
]
, replaces the toric varieties with arbitrary toric Deligne-Mumford stacks with semi-projective coarse moduli and the small quantum
D_{\hslash}
-modules with the big quantum
D_{\hslash}
-modules. Prior to this, Iritani
[
21
]
has proved DMC in less generality, using shift operators. We remark that it is the last work which inspired the formulation of Theorem
1.2
.

(2)

Much has been known about the case
P=B
. Generalizing Givental’s work
[
15
]
on the type A case, Kim
[
25
]
proved that
QH_{G\times\mathbb{G}_{m}}^{\bullet}(G/B)[q_{i}^{-1}|~i\in I]
is isomorphic to the
open quantum Toda lattice
constructed by Kostant
[
29
]
. We expect that after extension of scalars, the latter module is isomorphic to
G_{0}(X^{\vee}_{P},W,\pi,p)
. This will give an alternative (and shorter) proof of DMC for
G/B
.

(3)

Rietsch
[
45
]
proved that
\jac(X^{\vee}_{P},W,\pi,p)
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{O}(B_{e^{T}}^{\vee}\times_{G^{\vee}}U^{\vee}_{-}(\dot{w}_{P}\dot{w}_{0})^{-1}B^{\vee}_{-})
. Combined with the Peterson variety presentation which is proved recently by the author of the present paper
[
10
]
in full generality, it yields the isomorphism
\Phi_{mir}^{\hslash=0}
stated in Theorem
1.2
. See Section
1.2
for more details.

(4)

Katzarkov, Kontsevich and Pantev
[
24
]
proposed a version of DMC for arbitrary mirror pairs and interpreted it as a (conjectural) consequence of the homological mirror symmetry conjecture
[
27
]
.

(5)

For
G/P
an even dimensional quadric, Pech, Rietsch and Williams
[
42
]
constructed an explicit injective
D_{\hslash,Z(L^{\vee})}
-linear map from the A-model
D_{\hslash}
-module to the B-model
D_{\hslash}
-module.

(6)

For
G/P
an odd dimensional quadric, Pech and Rietsch
[
41
]
constructed an explicit injective
D_{\hslash,Z(L^{\vee})}
-linear map from the A-model
D_{\hslash}
-module to the B-model
D_{\hslash}
-module and proved that it is bijective when
\dim G/P=3
.

(7)

For
G/P
a Grassmannian, Marsh and Rietsch
[
37
]
constructed an explicit injective
D_{\hslash,Z(L^{\vee})}
-linear map from the A-model
D_{\hslash}
-module to the B-model
D_{\hslash}
-module.

(8)

Lam and Templier
[
33
]
proved DMC for minuscule flag varieties (Grassmannians and even dimensional quadrics, for example) based on a result from the geometric Langlands program, namely Zhu’s theorem
[
54
]
, which identifies Frenkel-Gross’
D
-module
[
13
]
with Heinloth-Ngô-Yun’s
D
-module
[
18
]
.

(9)

Hu
[
20
]
proved a big version of DMC for quadrics.

(10)

For
G=\mathbf{SL_{n}}
and
P
arbitrary, Li, Rietsch, Yang and Zhang
[
34
]
analyzed Rietsch’s version
[
44
,
45
]
of the ring isomorphism

\Phi_{mir}^{\hslash,h=0}:\jac(X^{\vee}_{P},W,\pi,p)_{h=0}\xrightarrow{\sim}QH^{\bullet}(G/P)[q_{i}^{-1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]

and proved that it sends
[W]
to the first Chern class
c_{1}(G/P)
. As a corollary, the eigenvalues of
c_{1}(G/P)\star-
are equal to the critical values of
W
.

2.
Notation

2.1.
Notation for
G

Let
G
be a simple simply-connected complex algebraic group. Fix a maximal torus
T
and a pair of Borel subgroups
B
and
B_{-}
such that
B\cap B_{-}=T
. Denote by
R
the set of roots associated to
(G,T)
, by
\{\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{r}\}\subset R
the fundamental system determined by
B
, and by
R^{+}
the set of roots which are positively spanned by
\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{r}
. For any
\alpha\in R
, denote by
\alpha^{\vee}
the coroot associated to
\alpha
. Let
\{\omega_{1},\ldots,\omega_{r}\}
be the dual basis of
\{\alpha^{\vee}_{1},\ldots,\alpha^{\vee}_{r}\}
, i.e. the fundamental weights. Denote by
W
the Weyl group and by
w_{0}
the longest element of
W
. Denote by
Q^{\vee}
the coroot lattice and by
Q^{\vee}_{+}\subset Q^{\vee}
the subset consisting of
\lambda
such that
\alpha(\lambda)\geqslant 0
for any
\alpha\in R^{+}
.

Fix a parabolic subgroup
P
of
G
which contains
B
. It is classified by a subset
I_{P}
of
I:=\{1,\ldots,r\}
. We have

\lie(P)=\lie(B)\oplus\bigoplus_{\alpha\in-R^{+}_{P}}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}

where
R_{P}^{+}:=R^{+}\cap\sum_{i\in I_{P}}\mathbb{Z}\cdot\alpha_{i}
. Denote by
W_{P}
the subgroup of
W
generated by simple reflections
s_{\alpha_{i}}
with
i\in I_{P}
, and by
w_{P}
the longest element of
W_{P}
. Define
Q^{\vee}_{P}:=\bigoplus_{i\in I_{P}}\mathbb{Z}\cdot\alpha^{\vee}_{i}\subseteq Q^{\vee}
.

Let
\mathcal{G}r:=G(\mathcal{K})/G(\mathcal{O})
be the affine Grassmannian of
G
where
\mathcal{K}:=\mathbb{C}((z))
and
\mathcal{O}:=\mathbb{C}[[z]]
. It has a
G(\mathcal{K})\rtimes\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action where
\mathbb{G}_{m}
corresponds to the loop rotation. Every
\lambda\in Q^{\vee}
is naturally a point of
G(\mathcal{K})
, and hence it gives rise to a point of
\mathcal{G}r
which we denote by
t^{\lambda}
. It is known that
\{t^{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in Q^{\vee}}
is the set of
T\times\mathbb{G}_{m}
-fixed points of
\mathcal{G}r
(recall
G
is assumed to be simply-connected). For any
\lambda\in Q^{\vee}_{+}
, define the spherical affine Schubert variety
\mathcal{G}r^{\leqslant\lambda}:=\overline{\mathcal{G}r^{\lambda}}
where
\mathcal{G}r^{\lambda}:=G(\mathcal{O})\cdot t^{\lambda}
. Define
\mathcal{B}:=\ev_{z=0}^{-1}(B_{-})
where
\ev_{z=0}:G(\mathcal{O})\rightarrow G
is the evaluation map at
z=0
. Let
W_{af}^{-}\subset W_{af}:=W\ltimes Q^{\vee}
be the set of minimal length coset representatives in
W_{af}/W
. Then the map
W_{af}^{-}\rightarrow Q^{\vee}
defined by
wt_{\lambda}\mapsto w(\lambda)
is bijective. For any
wt_{\lambda}\in W_{af}^{-}
, define the affine Schubert class

\xi_{wt_{\lambda}}:=\left[~\overline{\mathcal{B}\cdot t^{w(\lambda)}}~\right]\in H_{2\ell(wt_{\lambda})}^{T\times\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\mathcal{G}r).

It is known that
\{\xi_{wt_{\lambda}}\}_{wt_{\lambda}\in W_{af}^{-}}
is an
H_{T\times\mathbb{G}_{m}}^{\bullet}(\pt)
-basis of
H_{\bullet}^{T\times\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\mathcal{G}r)
.

Put
\widehat{T}:=T\times\mathbb{G}_{m}
and
\widehat{G}:=G\times\mathbb{G}_{m}
. There is a ring structure, called the
convolution product
, defined on
H_{\bullet}^{\widehat{G}}(\mathcal{G}r)

-\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{\mathcal{G}r}^{G}-:H_{\bullet}^{\widehat{G}}(\mathcal{G}r)\otimes_{H_{\widehat{G}}^{\bullet}(\pt)}H_{\bullet}^{\widehat{G}}(\mathcal{G}r)\rightarrow H_{\bullet}^{\widehat{G}}(\mathcal{G}r)

which recovers the
Pontryagin product
by forgetting the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action. It is characterized by the commutative diagram

H_{\bullet}^{\widehat{G}}(\mathcal{G}r)\otimes_{H_{\widehat{G}}^{\bullet}(\pt)}H_{\bullet}^{\widehat{G}}(\mathcal{G}r)
H_{\bullet}^{\widehat{G}}(\mathcal{G}r)
\left(\bigoplus_{\mu\in Q^{\vee}}\mathcal{R}_{\mu}\cdot[t^{\mu}]\right)\otimes_{\fof H_{\widehat{T}}^{\bullet}(\pt)}\left(\bigoplus_{\mu\in Q^{\vee}}\mathcal{R}_{\mu}\cdot[t^{\mu}]\right)
\bigoplus_{\mu\in Q^{\vee}}\mathcal{R}_{\mu}\cdot[t^{\mu}]
-\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{\mathcal{G}r}^{G}-
[t^{\mu_{1}}]\otimes[t^{\mu_{2}}]\mapsto[t^{\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}}]

where the vertical arrows are induced by localization and
\mathcal{R}_{\mu}:=\fof H_{\widehat{T}}^{\bullet}(\pt)
equipped with a
\fof H_{\widehat{T}}^{\bullet}(\pt)\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hslash]}\fof H_{\widehat{T}}^{\bullet}(\pt)
-module structure defined by letting the first factor act via the identity and the second factor act via the automorphism of the
\mathbb{C}[\hslash]
-algebra
H_{\widehat{T}}^{\bullet}(\pt)\simeq\mathbb{C}[\omega_{1},\ldots,\omega_{r},\hslash]

\twist_{-\mu}:f(\omega_{1},\ldots,\omega_{r},\hslash)\mapsto f(\omega_{1}-\omega_{1}(\mu)\hslash,\ldots,\omega_{r}-\omega_{r}(\mu)\hslash,\hslash).

(Notice that
H_{\bullet}^{\widehat{G}}(\mathcal{G}r)
is a module over
H_{\widehat{G}}^{\bullet}(\pt)\otimes H_{G}^{\bullet}(\pt)\simeq H_{\widehat{G}}^{\bullet}(\pt)\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\hslash]}H_{\widehat{G}}^{\bullet}(\pt)
where the first factor corresponds to the canonical
\widehat{G}
-action on
\mathcal{G}r
and the second corresponds to the canonical
G
-torsor on
\mathcal{G}r
.)

Define

-\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{\mathcal{G}r}^{T}-:H_{\bullet}^{\widehat{T}}(\mathcal{G}r)\otimes_{H_{\widehat{G}}^{\bullet}(\pt)}H_{\bullet}^{\widehat{G}}(\mathcal{G}r)\rightarrow H_{\bullet}^{\widehat{T}}(\mathcal{G}r)

to be the map obtained from
-\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{\mathcal{G}r}^{G}-
by extension of scalars.

2.2.
Notation for
G^{\vee}

Let
G^{\vee}
be the Langlands dual group of
G
, defined over
\mathbb{C}
, and
T^{\vee}
the maximal torus of
G^{\vee}
dual to
T
. The pair
(G^{\vee},T^{\vee})
is characterized by the property that the character (resp. cocharacter) lattice of
T^{\vee}
is equal to the cocharacter (resp. character) lattice of
T
, and the set of roots (resp. coroots) associated to
(G^{\vee},T^{\vee})
is equal to the set of coroots (resp. roots) associated to
(G,T)
. Since
G
is simply-connected,
G^{\vee}
is of adjoint type. Denote by
B^{\vee}
the Borel subgroup of
G^{\vee}
associated to the fundamental system
\{\alpha^{\vee}_{1},\ldots,\alpha^{\vee}_{r}\}
, and by
B_{-}^{\vee}
the Borel subgroup opposite to
B^{\vee}
. Denote by
U^{\vee}
(resp.
U^{\vee}_{-}
) the unipotent radical of
B^{\vee}
(resp.
B_{-}^{\vee}
). The Lie algebras of
G^{\vee}
,
T^{\vee}
,
B^{\vee}
,
B_{-}^{\vee}
,
U^{\vee}
,
U^{\vee}_{-}
are denoted by
\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
,
\mathfrak{t}^{\vee}
,
\mathfrak{b}^{\vee}
,
\mathfrak{b}^{\vee}_{-}
,
\mathfrak{n}^{\vee}
and
\mathfrak{n}^{\vee}_{-}
respectively.

Recall the parabolic subgroup
P
of
G
fixed in Section
2.1
. Let
P^{\vee}
be the parabolic subgroup of
G^{\vee}
satisfying

\lie(P^{\vee})=\lie(B^{\vee})\oplus\bigoplus_{\alpha\in-R^{+}_{P}}\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}_{\alpha^{\vee}}.

Denote by
Z(L^{\vee})\subseteq T^{\vee}
the center of the Levi subgroup
L^{\vee}
of
P^{\vee}
. It is the kernel of the homomorphism
(\alpha^{\vee}_{i})_{i\in I_{P}}:T^{\vee}\rightarrow(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{|I_{P}|}
of algebraic groups. Since
G^{\vee}
is of adjoint type, the morphism
(\alpha^{\vee}_{i}|_{Z(L^{\vee})})_{i\in I\setminus I_{P}}:Z(L^{\vee})\rightarrow(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{|I\setminus I_{P}|}
is an isomorphism, and hence it induces an isomorphism
\mathbb{C}[q_{i}^{\pm 1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{O}(Z(L^{\vee}))
of
\mathbb{C}
-algebras. Define

(2.1)

\mirror:\mathcal{O}(Z(L^{\vee}))\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{C}[q_{i}^{\pm 1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]

to be the inverse of the last isomorphism. (In the literature,
\spec(\mirror^{-1})
is called the
mirror map
.) For simplicity, we will sometimes identify these two algebras without mentioning
\mirror
.

For any
\lambda\in Q^{\vee}_{+}
, define
S(\lambda):=H^{0}(G^{\vee}/B^{\vee}_{-};G^{\vee}\times^{B_{-}^{\vee}}\mathbb{C}_{\lambda})
which is naturally a representation of
G^{\vee}
. By Borel-Weil’s theorem,
S(\lambda)
is irreducible and has highest weight
\lambda
. For each
\mu\in Q^{\vee}
, denote by
S(\lambda)_{\mu}
the
\mu
-weight space of
S(\lambda)
and by
S(\lambda)_{\leqslant\mu}
(resp.
S(\lambda)_{<\mu}
) the sum of
S(\lambda)_{\nu}
with
\nu\leqslant\mu
(resp.
\nu\leqslant\mu
and
\nu\neq\mu
). (Here
\nu\leqslant\mu
iff
\mu-\nu\in\sum_{i=1}^{r}\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}\cdot\alpha^{\vee}_{i}
.)

For any Lie algebra
\mathfrak{h}
, let
U_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{h})
be the asymptotic universal enveloping algebra of
\mathfrak{h}
and
Z_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{h})
the center of
U_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{h})
. A well-known result of Harish-Chandra says that
Z_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})
is isomorphic to
U_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})^{W}
as
\mathbb{C}[\hslash]
-algebras. Let us recall how the isomorphism is constructed. By PBW’s theorem, we have
U_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})=U_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})\oplus(\mathfrak{n}^{\vee}\cdot U_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})+U_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})\cdot\mathfrak{n}^{\vee}_{-})
as
\mathbb{C}[\hslash]
-modules. Hence every
z\in U_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})
can be expressed uniquely as the sum
z^{\prime}+z^{\prime\prime}
where
z^{\prime}\in U_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})
and
z^{\prime\prime}\in\mathfrak{n}^{\vee}\cdot U_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})+U_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})\cdot\mathfrak{n}^{\vee}_{-}
. (One can show that
z^{\prime\prime}
actually lies in
\mathfrak{n}^{\vee}\cdot U_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})\cap U_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})\cdot\mathfrak{n}^{\vee}_{-}
.) Let
\twist_{\rho^{\vee}}
be the automorphism of the
\mathbb{C}[\hslash]
-algebra
U_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})
defined by

\twist_{\rho^{\vee}}(x):=x+\hslash\rho^{\vee}(x),\quad x\in\mathfrak{t}^{\vee}

where
\rho^{\vee}
is the half-sum of the positive coroots. Define the
\hslash
-Harish-Chandra homomorphism

\begin{array}[]{ccccc}\Theta_{HC}&:&Z_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee})&\rightarrow&U_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})\\[5.0pt]
&&z&\mapsto&\twist_{\rho^{\vee}}(z^{\prime})\end{array}.

Then
\Theta_{HC}
is an injective homomorphism of
\mathbb{C}[\hslash]
-algebras whose image is equal to
U_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})^{W}
.

3.
A-model

3.1.
Quantum cohomology

Let
P
be the parabolic subgroup of
G
fixed in Section
2.1
. Let
H
be either
T
or
G
. Put
\widehat{H}:=H\times\mathbb{G}_{m}
. Consider the
\widehat{H}
-action on
G/P
where the
H
-action is the standard action and the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action is the trivial action. We will denote by
\hslash
the equivariant parameter for this extra
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action. Recall the
\widehat{H}
-equivariant quantum cohomology

QH_{\widehat{H}}^{\bullet}(G/P):=H_{\widehat{H}}^{\bullet}(G/P)\otimes\mathbb{C}[q_{i}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}].

Here, each
q_{i}
is the quantum parameter which corresponds to the effective curve class
\beta_{i}\in\pi_{2}(G/P)
satisfying
\langle c_{1}(L_{\rho}),\beta_{i}\rangle=\rho(\alpha^{\vee}_{i})
for any
\rho\in(Q^{\vee}/Q^{\vee}_{P})^{*}
where
L_{\rho}:=G\times^{P}\mathbb{C}_{-\rho}
. In later sections, we will identify
\mathbb{C}[q_{i}^{\pm 1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]
with the group algebra
\mathbb{C}[Q^{\vee}/Q^{\vee}_{P}]
via
q_{i}\mapsto q^{[\alpha^{\vee}_{i}]}
. Grade
QH_{\widehat{H}}^{\bullet}(G/P)
by requiring each
q_{i}
to have degree
2\langle c_{1}(G/P),\beta_{i}\rangle=2\sum_{\alpha\in R^{+}\setminus R^{+}_{P}}\alpha(\alpha^{\vee}_{i})
. It is well-known (see e.g.
[
12
]
) that
QH_{\widehat{H}}^{\bullet}(G/P)
is a graded commutative
H_{\widehat{H}}^{\bullet}(\pt)
-algebra where the algebra structure, called the
\widehat{H}
-equivariant quantum cup product
, is defined by

y_{i}\star y_{j}:=\sum_{k}\sum_{(d_{i})_{i\in I\setminus I_{P}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 0}^{|I\setminus I_{P}|}}\left(\prod_{i\in I\setminus I_{P}}q_{i}^{d_{i}}\right)\left(\int_{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,3}(G/P,\beta_{\mathbf{d}})}\ev_{1}^{*}y_{i}\cup\ev_{2}^{*}y_{j}\cup ev_{3}^{*}y^{k}\right)y_{k}.

Here,
\beta_{\mathbf{d}}:=\sum_{i\in I\setminus I_{P}}d_{i}\beta_{i}
and
\{y_{i}\}
,
\{y^{i}\}
are any
H_{\widehat{H}}^{\bullet}(\pt)
-bases of
H_{\widehat{H}}^{\bullet}(G/P)
which are dual to each other with respect to the pairing
\int_{G/P}-\cup-
. For
H=T
, we will take
\{y_{i}\}
and
\{y^{i}\}
to be the
Schubert basis

\{\sigma_{v}\}_{v\in W^{P}}
and the
opposite Schubert basis

\{\sigma^{v}\}_{v\in W^{P}}
defined by

\displaystyle\sigma_{v}

\displaystyle:=\pd\left[\overline{B_{-}\dot{v}P/P}\right]\in H_{\widehat{T}}^{2\ell(v)}(G/P)

\displaystyle\sigma^{v}

\displaystyle:=\pd\left[\overline{B\dot{v}P/P}\right]\in H_{\widehat{T}}^{\dim_{\mathbb{R}}(G/P)-2\ell(v)}(G/P)

where
W^{P}
is the set of minimal length coset representatives in
W/W_{P}
.

3.2.
Quantum
\hslash
-connection

Let
H
be either
T
or
G
.

Definition 3.1
.

Define the
quantum
\hslash
-connection

\nabla^{H}:QH_{\widehat{H}}^{\bullet}(G/P)[q_{i}^{-1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]\rightarrow\Omega^{1}_{\spec\mathbb{C}[q_{i}^{\pm 1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[q_{i}^{\pm 1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]}QH_{\widehat{H}}^{\bullet}(G/P)[q_{i}^{-1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]

by

\nabla^{H}:=\sum_{i\in I\setminus I_{P}}dq_{i}\otimes\nabla^{H}_{\partial_{q_{i}}}

with

\nabla^{H}_{\partial_{q_{i}}}:=\hslash\partial_{q_{i}}+q_{i}^{-1}c_{1}^{\widehat{H}}(L_{\omega_{i}})\star-

where
\omega_{i}
is the
i
-th fundamental weight and the canonical
\widehat{H}
-linearization of
L_{\omega_{i}}:=G\times^{P}\mathbb{C}_{-\omega_{i}}
is used.

It is well-known (see e.g.
[
12
,
36
]
) that
\nabla^{H}
satisfies

\nabla^{H}_{X}\circ\nabla^{H}_{Y}-\nabla^{H}_{Y}\circ\nabla^{H}_{X}=\hslash\nabla^{H}_{[X,Y]}

for any
X,Y\in\mathfrak{X}(\spec\mathbb{C}[q_{i}^{\pm 1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}])
. It follows that
\nabla^{H}
defines a
D_{\hslash,\spec\mathbb{C}[q_{i}^{\pm 1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]}
-module structure on
QH_{\widehat{H}}^{\bullet}(G/P)[q_{i}^{-1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]
.

3.3.
Shift operators

We recall shift operators arising from
[
8
,
22
,
35
,
38
,
40
]
. The reader is recommended to read this subsection after reading Section
5.1
and Section
5.2
where we will introduce a more general version.

For any
\lambda\in Q^{\vee}
, define an automorphism
\twist_{\lambda}
of the
\mathbb{C}[\hslash]
-algebra
H_{\widehat{T}}^{\bullet}(\pt)
by

(3.1)

\twist_{\lambda}(f(\omega_{1},\ldots,\omega_{r},\hslash)):=f(\omega_{1}+\omega_{1}(\lambda)\hslash,\ldots,\omega_{r}+\omega_{r}(\lambda)\hslash,\hslash)

for any
f(\omega_{1},\ldots,\omega_{r},\hslash)\in\mathbb{C}[\omega_{1},\ldots,\omega_{r},\hslash]\simeq H_{\widehat{T}}^{\bullet}(\pt)
. For an
H_{\widehat{T}}^{\bullet}(\pt)
-module
N
, denote by
\twist_{\lambda}(N)
the
H_{\widehat{T}}^{\bullet}(\pt)
-module
N
with module structure twisted by
\twist_{\lambda}
.

Definition 3.2
.

Let
N_{1}
and
N_{2}
be
H_{\widehat{T}}^{\bullet}(\pt)
-modules and
\phi:N_{1}\rightarrow N_{2}
a
\mathbb{C}[\hslash]
-linear map. Let
\lambda\in Q^{\vee}
. We say that
\phi
is
\lambda
-twisted
H_{\widehat{T}}^{\bullet}(\pt)
-linear
if it is
H_{\widehat{T}}^{\bullet}(\pt)
-linear as a map from
N_{1}
to
\twist_{\lambda}(N_{2})
.

Definition 3.3
.

Let
\lambda\in Q^{\vee}
. Define

\widetilde{\mathbb{S}}^{A}_{\lambda}:QH_{\widehat{G}}^{\bullet}(G/P)[q_{i}^{-1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]\rightarrow QH_{\widehat{T}}^{\bullet}(G/P)[q_{i}^{-1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]

by

\widetilde{\mathbb{S}}^{A}_{\lambda}(y):=[t^{\lambda}]\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{GMP}^{T}y

for any
y\in QH_{\widehat{G}}^{\bullet}(G/P)[q_{i}^{-1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]
, where
-\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{GMP}^{T}-
will be defined in Definition
5.5
.

Lemma 3.4
.

(Shift operators)

\widetilde{\mathbb{S}}^{A}_{\lambda}
extends uniquely to a
(-\lambda)
-twisted
H_{\widehat{T}}^{\bullet}(\pt)
-linear map

\mathbb{S}^{A}_{\lambda}:QH_{\widehat{T}}^{\bullet}(G/P)[q_{i}^{-1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]\rightarrow QH_{\widehat{T}}^{\bullet}(G/P)[q_{i}^{-1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}].

Proof.

All notations used below can be found in Section
5.1
. By (
5.2
), we have

\widetilde{\mathbb{S}}^{A}_{\lambda}(y)=\sum_{v\in W^{P}}\sum_{\eta\in Q^{\vee}/Q^{\vee}_{P}}q^{\eta}\mathcal{I}(t^{\lambda},[t^{\lambda}],y,v,\eta)\sigma_{v}

where

\mathcal{I}(t^{\lambda},[t^{\lambda}],y,v,\eta):=\int_{[\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{0,\infty}(t^{\lambda},\eta)]^{vir}}\ev_{t^{\lambda},\eta,0}^{*}m_{t^{\lambda}}([t^{\lambda}]\otimes y)\cup\ev_{t^{\lambda},\eta,\infty}^{*}\sigma^{v}.

We have

\mathcal{E}_{t^{\lambda}}(G/P)\simeq\left(\mathbb{A}^{1}_{z}\times G/P\times\{0,\infty\}\right)/_{(z,x,0)\sim(z^{-1},\lambda(z)\cdot x,\infty)},

and
\widehat{T}
acts on
\mathcal{E}_{t^{\lambda}}(G/P)
in the following way

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}(t,\zeta)\cdot[z,x,0]&=&[\zeta z,t\lambda(\zeta)^{-1}\cdot x,0]\\[5.0pt]
(t,\zeta)\cdot[z,x,\infty]&=&[\zeta^{-1}z,t\cdot x,\infty]\end{array}\right.

for any
t\in T
,
\zeta\in\mathbb{G}_{m}
,
z\in\mathbb{A}^{1}
and
x\in G/P
. It follows that there is a canonical isomorphism
D_{t^{\lambda},0}\simeq G/P
which is
\widehat{T}
-equivariant if
\widehat{T}
acts on
G/P
in the following way

(t,\zeta)\cdot x=t\lambda(\zeta)^{-1}\cdot x\qquad t\in T,~\zeta\in\mathbb{G}_{m},~x\in G/P.

Hence there is a unique isomorphism of
H_{\widehat{T}}^{\bullet}(\pt)
-modules

(3.2)

H_{\widehat{T}}^{\bullet}(G/P)\xrightarrow{\sim}\twist_{-\lambda}(H_{\widehat{T}}^{\bullet}(D_{t^{\lambda},0}))

sending the fundamental class of each torus fixed point of
G/P
to the fundamental class of the corresponding torus fixed point of
D_{t^{\lambda},0}
. It is straightforward to see that
m_{t^{\lambda}}([t^{\lambda}]\otimes-)
is the restriction of (
3.2
) to
H_{\widehat{G}}^{\bullet}(G/P)
. Therefore, this map, and hence
\widetilde{\mathbb{S}}^{A}_{\lambda}
, extends to a
(-\lambda)
-twisted
H_{\widehat{T}}^{\bullet}(\pt)
-linear map as desired.
∎

Remark 3.5
.

Up to a constant multiple,
\mathbb{S}^{A}_{\lambda}
is equal to the operator defined in
[
22
, Definition 3.9]
. See the proof of
[
9
, Lemma 3.14]
for an explanation of this multiple.

Lemma 3.6
.

(
[
22
, Corollary 3.16]
) We have
\mathbb{S}^{A}_{0}=\id
and

\mathbb{S}^{A}_{\lambda_{1}}\circ\mathbb{S}^{A}_{\lambda_{2}}=\mathbb{S}^{A}_{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\qquad\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\in Q^{\vee}.

In particular, each
\mathbb{S}^{A}_{\lambda}
is invertible.
\square

Lemma 3.7
.

(
[
22
, Corollary 3.15]
)
For any
\lambda\in Q^{\vee}
and
i\in I\setminus I_{P}
,
\mathbb{S}^{A}_{\lambda}
commutes with
\nabla^{T}_{\partial_{q_{i}}}
.
\square

4.
B-model

4.1.
Rietsch mirror

We recall the definition of the Rietsch mirror. Our version is close to the one defined in
[
33
, Section 6]
. See also
[
45
, Section 4]
and
[
52
, Section 6.6]
.

Fix generators
e^{\vee}_{i}
(
1\leqslant i\leqslant r
) of
\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}_{\alpha^{\vee}_{i}}
and let
f^{\vee}_{i}
be the generators of
\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}_{-\alpha^{\vee}_{i}}
satisfying
[e^{\vee}_{i},f^{\vee}_{i}]=\alpha_{i}
. Denote by
x_{i},y_{i}:\mathbb{G}_{a}\rightarrow G^{\vee}
the unique group homomorphisms satisfying
\lie(x_{i})(1)=e^{\vee}_{i}
and
\lie(y_{i})(1)=f^{\vee}_{i}
. Define

\dot{s}_{\alpha_{i}}:=y_{i}(-1)x_{i}(1)y_{i}(-1).

It is well-known that
\dot{s}_{\alpha_{i}}\in N(T^{\vee})
and represents
s_{\alpha_{i}}\in W\simeq N(T^{\vee})/T^{\vee}
. Moreover, these elements satisfy the standard braid relations so that we can define
\dot{w}\in N(T^{\vee})
for any
w\in W
which represents
w\in W
such that
\dot{w}=\dot{s}_{\alpha_{i_{1}}}\cdots\dot{s}_{\alpha_{i_{\ell(w)}}}
whenever
w=s_{\alpha_{i_{1}}}\cdots s_{\alpha_{i_{\ell(w)}}}
is a reduced word decomposition.

There is a unique character

(4.1)

\chi:\mathfrak{n}^{\vee}_{-}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}

satisfying
\chi(f^{\vee}_{i})=1
for any
1\leqslant i\leqslant r
. It gives rise to a group homomorphism

e^{\chi}:U^{\vee}_{-}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}_{a}.

We can also define
\chi
in the following equivalent way. Let
Kil(-,-)
be the Killing form of
\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}
and
\alpha_{0}
the highest positive root. Define

(4.2)

\beta(-,-):=\frac{2Kil(-,-)}{Kil(\alpha_{0},\alpha_{0})}\quad\text{ and }\quad e:=\sum_{i=1}^{r}|\alpha^{\vee}_{i}|^{2}e_{i}^{\vee}

where
|\alpha^{\vee}_{i}|^{2}:=Kil(\alpha_{0},\alpha_{0})/Kil(\alpha_{i},\alpha_{i})=2\beta(\alpha_{i},\alpha_{i})^{-1}
. Then we have

\chi=\beta(e,-)|_{\mathfrak{n}^{\vee}_{-}}.

Remark 4.1
.

Notice that
\dot{w}
and
\chi
, and hence the Rietsch mirror we are going to define, depend on the generators
e_{i}^{\vee}
. But since any two sets of generators are
\adjoint
-related to each other, the Rietsch mirror does not depend on them up to isomorphism. For convenience, we will take
e_{i}^{\vee}
to be
x_{i}
from
[
53
, Proposition 5.6]
or
e_{\alpha^{\vee}_{i}}
from
[
10
, Section 4.1]
. Roughly speaking, these generators are characterized by the property that the element
e:=\sum_{i=1}^{r}|\alpha^{\vee}_{i}|^{2}e_{i}^{\vee}
corresponds to the cup product by the positive generator of
H^{2}(\mathcal{G}r;\mathbb{Z})
via the geometric Satake equivalence
[
39
]
.

Definition 4.2
.

The
Rietsch mirror
of
G/P
is a quintuple
(X^{\vee}_{P},W,\pi,p,\omega_{X^{\vee}_{P}})
where

(1)

X^{\vee}_{P}:=\{(g,t)\in G^{\vee}\times Z(L^{\vee})|~g\in B^{\vee}\cap U^{\vee}_{-}(\dot{w}_{P}\dot{w}_{0})^{-1}tU^{\vee}_{-}\};

(2)

W:X^{\vee}_{P}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1},\quad\pi:X^{\vee}_{P}\rightarrow Z(L^{\vee}),\quad p:X^{\vee}_{P}\rightarrow T^{\vee}

are morphisms defined by

W(x):=e^{\chi}(u_{1})+e^{\chi}(u_{2}),\quad\pi(x):=t,\quad p(x):=t_{0}

for any
x=(g,t)\in X^{\vee}_{P}
with

g=u_{0}t_{0}=u_{1}(\dot{w}_{P}\dot{w}_{0})^{-1}tu_{2}

(
u_{0}\in U^{\vee}
,
t_{0}\in T^{\vee}
,
u_{1},u_{2}\in U^{\vee}_{-}
); and

(3)

\omega_{X^{\vee}_{P}}\in\Omega^{top}(X^{\vee}_{P}/Z(L^{\vee}))

is a fiberwise volume form with respect to
\pi
whose definition is postponed to Definition
4.11
in Section
4.4
.

Remark 4.3
.

Notice that the point
t_{0}
from Definition
4.2
(2) is unique, and hence
p
is well-defined. On the other hand,
u_{1}
and
u_{2}
are not unique. But one can show that
e^{\chi}(u_{1})+e^{\chi}(u_{2})
depends only on
x
, and hence
W
is also well-defined.

Remark 4.4
.

Lam and Templier
[
33
]
defined the Rietsch mirror to be Berenstein-Kazhdan’s
parabolic geometric crystal

[
5
]
. In Appendix
D
, we will show that these two definitions are equivalent.

Define

\mathcal{R}_{P}:=U^{\vee}B_{-}^{\vee}/B_{-}^{\vee}\cap U^{\vee}_{-}(\dot{w}_{P}\dot{w}_{0})^{-1}B_{-}^{\vee}/B_{-}^{\vee}\subseteq G^{\vee}/B^{\vee}_{-}.

Let
P_{-}^{\vee}
be the parabolic subgroup of
G^{\vee}
satisfying

\lie(P_{-}^{\vee})=\lie(B_{-}^{\vee})\oplus\bigoplus_{\alpha\in R^{+}_{P}}\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}_{\alpha^{\vee}}.

It is not difficult to see that the restriction of the projection
G^{\vee}/B^{\vee}_{-}\rightarrow G^{\vee}/P_{-}^{\vee}
to
\mathcal{R}_{P}
is an isomorphism onto its image which is an open subscheme of
G^{\vee}/P_{-}^{\vee}
. We denote the image by
\mathcal{U}_{P}
. In the literature,
\mathcal{R}_{P}
(resp.
\mathcal{U}_{P}
) is called an open (resp. open projected) Richardson variety.

Lemma 4.5
.

The morphism
G^{\vee}\times Z(L^{\vee})\rightarrow G^{\vee}/P_{-}^{\vee}\times Z(L^{\vee})
defined by
(g,t)\mapsto(gP_{-}^{\vee},t)
induces an isomorphism

\nu:X^{\vee}_{P}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{U}_{P}\times Z(L^{\vee})

of
Z(L^{\vee})
-schemes.

Proof.

The proof is straightforward and left to the reader.
∎

Finally, we define a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
X^{\vee}_{P}
as in
[
33
, Section 6.21]
. Let
\mathbb{G}_{m}
act on

•

G^{\vee}
via the conjugate action by the cocharacter
2\rho=\sum_{\alpha\in R^{+}}\alpha
;

•

Z(L^{\vee})
via the multiplication by the cocharacter
-2\sum_{\alpha\in R^{+}\setminus R_{P}^{+}}\alpha
;

•

T^{\vee}
via the trivial action; and

•

\mathbb{A}^{1}
via the multiplication of weight
-2
.

Lemma 4.6
.

The
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
G^{\vee}\times Z(L^{\vee})
induces a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
X^{\vee}_{P}
such that
W
,
\pi
and
p
are equivariant.

Proof.

The proof is similar to the proof of
[
33
, Proposition 6.24]
.
∎

4.2.
Brieskorn lattice

We define the Brieskorn lattice associated to the quadruple
(X^{\vee}_{P},W,\pi,p)
defined in Definition
4.2
. (The fiberwise volume form
\omega_{X^{\vee}_{P}}
is not needed here.) It is the zeroth cohomology of
WCr^{1/\hslash}_{(G,P)}
defined in
[
33
]
. See also
[
37
,
41
,
42
]
.

Definition 4.7
.

(1)

Define the
Brieskorn lattice
associated to
(X^{\vee}_{P},W,\pi,p)

\quad G_{0}(X^{\vee}_{P},W,\pi,p):=\coker\left(U_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})\otimes\Omega^{top-1}(X^{\vee}_{P}/Z(L^{\vee}))\xrightarrow{\partial}U_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})\otimes\Omega^{top}(X^{\vee}_{P}/Z(L^{\vee}))\right)

where

•

\Omega^{i}(X^{\vee}_{P}/Z(L^{\vee}))
is the space of relative
i
-forms on
X^{\vee}_{P}
with respect to
\pi
; and

•

\partial
is defined by

(4.3)

\qquad\qquad\quad\partial(z\otimes\omega):=z\otimes(\hslash d\omega+dW\wedge\omega)-\sum_{i}zh_{i}\otimes(p^{*}\langle h^{i},\mccccc_{T^{\vee}}\rangle)\wedge\omega

where
\{h_{i}\}\subset\mathfrak{t}^{\vee}
and
\{h^{i}\}\subset(\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})^{*}
are dual bases and
\mccccc_{T^{\vee}}\in\Omega^{1}(T^{\vee};\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})
is the Maurer-Cartan form of
T^{\vee}
.

(2)

Define the
Gauss-Manin
\hslash
-connection
on
G_{0}(X^{\vee}_{P},W,\pi,p)
which is a
D_{\hslash,Z(L^{\vee})}
-module structure by the formula

\eta\cdot[z\otimes\omega]:=\left[z\otimes(\hslash\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{\eta}}\omega+(\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{\eta}}W)\omega)-\sum_{i}zh_{i}\otimes(\iota_{\widetilde{\eta}}p^{*}\langle h^{i},\mccccc_{T^{\vee}}\rangle)\omega\right]

for any
\eta\in\mathfrak{X}(Z(L^{\vee}))
and
[z\otimes\omega]\in G_{0}(X^{\vee}_{P},W,\pi,p)
where
\widetilde{\eta}\in\mathfrak{X}(X^{\vee}_{P})
is a lift of
\eta
.

We define a grading on
G_{0}(X^{\vee}_{P},W,\pi,p)
as in
[
33
, Section 11]
. Recall the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
X^{\vee}_{P}
(see Lemma
4.6
). Since
\pi
is equivariant, the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
X^{\vee}_{P}
induces a linear
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action, and hence a grading, on
\Omega^{\bullet}(X^{\vee}_{P}/Z(L^{\vee}))
. (Warning: we do not take the de Rham degree into account.) Grade
U_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})
by requiring
\hslash
and every element of
\mathfrak{t}^{\vee}
to have degree 2. Then
U_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})\otimes\Omega^{\bullet}(X^{\vee}_{P}/Z(L^{\vee}))
has a grading. Consider
\partial
defined in (
4.3
). Since
W
is equivariant,
dW
has degree 2, and since
p
is equivariant,
p^{*}\langle h^{i},\mccccc_{T^{\vee}}\rangle
has degree 0. It follows that
\partial(z\otimes\omega)
is homogeneous whenever
z\otimes\omega
is. We have proved

Lemma 4.8
.

The grading on
U_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})\otimes\Omega^{top}(X^{\vee}_{P}/Z(L^{\vee}))
induces a grading on
G_{0}(X^{\vee}_{P},W,\pi,p)
.
\square

4.3.
Jacobi ring

Definition 4.9
.

Define the
Jacobi ring

\jac(X^{\vee}_{P},W,\pi,p)
to be the coordinate ring of the scheme-theoretic zero locus of the relative 1-form

\pr_{X^{\vee}_{P}}^{*}dW-\langle\pr_{\mathfrak{t}},(p\circ\pr_{X^{\vee}_{P}})^{*}\mccccc_{T^{\vee}}\rangle\in\Omega^{1}(X^{\vee}_{P}\times\mathfrak{t}/Z(L^{\vee})\times\mathfrak{t})

where
\pr_{X^{\vee}_{P}}:X^{\vee}_{P}\times\mathfrak{t}\rightarrow X^{\vee}_{P}
and
\pr_{\mathfrak{t}}:X^{\vee}_{P}\times\mathfrak{t}\rightarrow\mathfrak{t}
are the projections.

Let
\mathcal{F}_{\hslash=0}
denote the functor
N\mapsto N/\hslash N
.

Lemma 4.10
.

Every fiberwise volume form on
X^{\vee}_{P}
induces an isomorphism

\jac(X^{\vee}_{P},W,\pi,p)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{F}_{\hslash=0}(G_{0}(X^{\vee}_{P},W,\pi,p))

of
\sym^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})\otimes\mathcal{O}(Z(L^{\vee}))
-modules.

Proof.

Identify
\Omega^{top}(X^{\vee}_{P}/Z(L^{\vee}))
with
\mathcal{O}(X^{\vee}_{P})
using the given fiberwise volume form. By (
4.3
), the
\sym^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})\otimes\mathcal{O}(Z(L^{\vee}))
-module
\mathcal{F}_{\hslash=0}(G_{0}(X^{\vee}_{P},W,\pi,p))
is isomorphic to the quotient of
\sym^{\bullet}(\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})\otimes\mathcal{O}(X^{\vee}_{P})
by the ideal generated by elements of the form

1\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\zeta_{j}}W-\sum_{i}h_{i}\otimes\iota_{\zeta_{j}}p^{*}\langle h^{i},\mccccc_{T^{\vee}}\rangle

where
\{\zeta_{j}\}
is a global frame of
X^{\vee}_{P}
relative to
Z(L^{\vee})
. It is clear that the latter module is in fact equal to
\jac(X^{\vee}_{P},W,\pi,p)
.
∎

4.4.
Fiberwise volume form

We finish the definition of the Rietsch mirror (Definition
4.2
) by defining the fiberwise volume form
\omega_{X^{\vee}_{P}}
on
X^{\vee}_{P}
.

Recall the open projected Richardson variety
\mathcal{U}_{P}\subseteq G^{\vee}/P_{-}^{\vee}
defined in Section
4.1
(before Lemma
4.5
). By
[
26
, Lemma 5.4]
, the complement
D:=(G^{\vee}/P_{-}^{\vee})\setminus\mathcal{U}_{P}
has pure codimension one, and there exists a volume form
\omega_{\mathcal{U}_{P}}
on
\mathcal{U}_{P}
(unique up to scalar) which has simple pole along every irreducible component of
D
.

Definition 4.11
.

(Definition
4.2
continued) Define
\omega_{X^{\vee}_{P}}\in\Omega^{top}(X^{\vee}_{P}/Z(L^{\vee}))
by

\omega_{X^{\vee}_{P}}:=(\pr_{\mathcal{U}_{P}}\circ\nu)^{*}\omega_{\mathcal{U}_{P}}

where
\nu:X^{\vee}_{P}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{U}_{P}\times Z(L^{\vee})
is the isomorphism from Lemma
4.5
and
\pr_{\mathcal{U}_{P}}:\mathcal{U}_{P}\times Z(L^{\vee})\rightarrow\mathcal{U}_{P}
is the projection.

Remark 4.12
.

Notice that
\omega_{X^{\vee}_{P}}
is only defined up to scalar. It can be normalized by choosing an orientation of an integration cycle. See Remark
4.13
below. But for our purpose, such normalization is unnecessary.

Remark 4.13
.

The above definition is due to Lam and Templier
[
33
, Section 6.6]
. See also
[
37
, Section 8]
for the case of Grassmannian. It is a priori different from the one by Rietsch
[
45
, Section 7]
which uses, instead of
\omega_{\mathcal{U}_{P}}
, the unique (up to sign) volume form whose restrictions to every torus chart is equal to the standard volume form
\pm dt_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge dt_{N}/t_{1}\cdots t_{N}
. But Lam
[
30
, Lemma 2.9]
proved that these two definitions are the same (up to scalar). (Strictly speaking, he only dealt with the case
P=B
but his proof clearly works for the general case.)

Lemma 4.14
.

The degree of
[\omega_{X^{\vee}_{P}}]
with respect to the grading from Lemma
4.8
is 0.

Proof.

This can be proved in three ways. First, it follows immediately from
[
33
, Lemma 6.26]
. Second, by Remark
4.13
,
\omega_{X^{\vee}_{P}}
is locally equal to a scalar multiple of
dt_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge dt_{N}/t_{1}\cdots t_{N}
which is easily seen to have degree 0. Third, we have
\omega_{X^{\vee}_{P}}=\overline{f}^{-1}\overline{\omega}_{\mathcal{U}}
where
\overline{f}
and
\overline{\omega}_{\mathcal{U}}
come from the proof of Lemma
A.1
. The result can be proved by computing their degrees directly. Details are left to the reader.
∎

4.5.
Shift operators

Following
[
21
, Proposition 3.20]
, we define shift operators for the Brieskorn lattice
G_{0}(X^{\vee}_{P},W,\pi,p)
as follows.

For any
\lambda\in Q^{\vee}
, define an automorphism
\twist_{\lambda}
of the
\mathbb{C}[\hslash]
-algebra
U_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})
by

\twist_{\lambda}(x):=x+\hslash\lambda(x),\quad x\in\mathfrak{t}^{\vee}.

For a
U_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})
-module
N
, denote by
\twist_{\lambda}(N)
the
U_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})
-module
N
with module structure twisted by
\twist_{\lambda}
.

Definition 4.15
.

Let
N_{1}
and
N_{2}
be
U_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})
-modules and
\phi:N_{1}\rightarrow N_{2}
a
\mathbb{C}[\hslash]
-linear map. Let
\lambda\in Q^{\vee}
. We say that
\phi
is
\lambda
-twisted
U_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})
-linear
if it is
U_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})
-linear as a map from
N_{1}
to
\twist_{\lambda}(N_{2})
.

Definition 4.16
.

Let
\lambda\in Q^{\vee}
. Define

\widetilde{\mathbb{S}}^{B}_{\lambda}:U_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})\otimes\Omega^{top}(X^{\vee}_{P}/Z(L^{\vee}))\rightarrow U_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})\otimes\Omega^{top}(X^{\vee}_{P}/Z(L^{\vee}))

by

\widetilde{\mathbb{S}}^{B}_{\lambda}(z\otimes\omega):=\twist_{-\lambda}(z)\otimes(\lambda\circ p)\omega

for any
z\in U_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})
and
\omega\in\Omega^{top}(X^{\vee}_{P}/Z(L^{\vee}))
. It is
(-\lambda)
-twisted
U_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})
-linear in the sense of Definition
4.15
.

Lemma 4.17
.

(Shift operators)

\widetilde{\mathbb{S}}^{B}_{\lambda}
descends to a
(-\lambda)
-twisted
U_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})
-linear map

\mathbb{S}^{B}_{\lambda}:G_{0}(X^{\vee}_{P},W,\pi,p)\rightarrow G_{0}(X^{\vee}_{P},W,\pi,p)

Proof.

It suffices to show

\widetilde{\mathbb{S}}^{B}_{\lambda}(\partial(z\otimes\omega))=\partial(\twist_{-\lambda}(z)\otimes(\lambda\circ p)\omega)

for any
z\in U_{\hslash}(\mathfrak{t}^{\vee})
and
\omega\in\Omega^{top-1}(X^{\vee}_{P}/Z(L^{\vee}))
, where
\partial
is defined in (
4.3
). We have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{S}}^{B}_{\lambda}(\partial(z\otimes\omega))=~

\displaystyle~\twist_{-\lambda}(z)\otimes(\hslash(\lambda\circ p)d\omega+(\lambda\circ p)dW\wedge\omega)

\displaystyle~-\sum_{i}\twist_{-\lambda}(zh_{i})\otimes(\lambda\circ p)(p^{*}\langle h^{i},\mccccc_{T^{\vee}}\rangle)\wedge\omega

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle~\partial(\twist_{-\lambda}(z)\otimes(\lambda\circ p)\omega)-\hslash\twist_{-\lambda}(z)\otimes d(\lambda\circ p)\wedge\omega

\displaystyle~+\sum_{i}\twist_{-\lambda}(z)(h_{i}-\twist_{-\lambda}(h_{i}))\otimes(\lambda\circ p)(p^{*}\langle h^{i},\mccccc_{T^{\vee}}\rangle)\wedge\omega

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle~\partial(\twist_{-\lambda}(z)\otimes(\lambda\circ p)\omega)

\displaystyle~-\hslash\twist_{-\lambda}(z)\otimes(d(\lambda\circ p)-(\lambda\circ p)p^{*}\langle\lambda,\mccccc_{T^{\vee}}\rangle)\wedge\omega.

It is not difficult to see that
d(\lambda\circ p)=(\lambda\circ p)p^{*}\langle\lambda,\mccccc_{T^{\vee}}\rangle
, and hence the last expression is equal to
\partial(\twist_{-\lambda}(z)\otimes(\lambda\circ p)\omega)
as desired.
∎

The following are the parallel properties of the A-model shift operators from Section
3.3
.

Lemma 4.18
.

We have
\mathbb{S}^{B}_{0}=\id
and

\mathbb{S}^{B}_{\lambda_{1}}\circ\mathbb{S}^{B}_{\lambda_{2}}=\mathbb{S}^{B}_{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}}\qquad\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\in Q^{\vee}.

In particular, each
\mathbb{S}^{B}_{\lambda}
is invertible.

Proof.

This is clear from definition.
∎

Lemma 4.19
.

For any
\lambda\in Q^{\vee}
and
i\in I\setminus I_{P}
,
\mathbb{S}^{B}_{\lambda}
is
D_{\hslash,Z(L^{\vee})}
-linear.

Proof.

This is straightforward. Since we will not use this result, we omit the details.
∎

5.
Non-abelian shift operators

Non-abelian shift operators
are defined by González, Mak and Pomerleano
[
17
]
in the symplectic category. They generalize
abelian shift operators
(see Section
3.3
) and
Savelyev-Seidel’s homomorphisms

[
48
,
49
,
50
,
51
]
simultaneously. In this section, we give an exposition in the algebraic category, where the target space is restricted to
G/P
. We also prove some computational results based on
[
9
,
10
]
.

5.1.
G/P
-bundles and moduli of sections

By Beauville-Laszlo’s theorem
[
4
]
, the affine Grassmannian
\mathcal{G}r
parametrizes
G
-torsors over
\mathbb{P}^{1}
with a trivialization over
\mathbb{P}^{1}\setminus 0
. In other words, every morphism
f:\Gamma\rightarrow\mathcal{G}r
is represented by a
G
-torsor
\mathcal{E}_{f}
over
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\Gamma
with a trivialization
\nu_{f}:\mathcal{E}_{f}|_{(\mathbb{P}^{1}\setminus 0)\times\Gamma}\xrightarrow{\sim}(\mathbb{P}^{1}\setminus 0)\times\Gamma\times G
. In what follows, we assume
\Gamma
is a smooth projective variety. Then the associated fiber bundle

\mathcal{E}_{f}(G/P):=\mathcal{E}_{f}\times^{G}G/P

exists as a smooth projective variety. Denote by
\pi_{f}:\mathcal{E}_{f}(G/P)\rightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\Gamma
the projection. Define

D_{f,0}:=\pi_{f}^{-1}(0\times\Gamma)\quad\text{ and }\quad D_{f,\infty}:=\pi_{f}^{-1}(\infty\times\Gamma)

which are smooth divisors on
\mathcal{E}_{f}(G/P)
. Denote by
\iota_{f,0/\infty}:D_{f,0/\infty}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}_{f}(G/P)
the inclusions. The trivialization
\nu_{f}
induces an isomorphism
D_{f,\infty}\simeq\Gamma\times G/P
. Let
\rho\in(Q^{\vee}/Q^{\vee}_{P})^{*}
. Define
L_{\rho}:=G\times^{P}\mathbb{C}_{-\rho}
which is a line bundle on
G/P
. Since
L_{\rho}
has a (unique)
G
-linearization, there is a natural line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\rho}
on
\mathcal{E}_{f}(G/P)
, constructed by descent, whose restriction to every fiber of
\pi_{f}
is isomorphic to
L_{\rho}
.

Definition 5.1
.

Let
f:\Gamma\rightarrow\mathcal{G}r
be a morphism and
\eta\in Q^{\vee}/Q^{\vee}_{P}
.

(1)

Define

\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{0,\infty}(f,\eta):=\bigcup_{\beta}~\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,2}(\mathcal{E}_{f}(G/P),\beta)\times_{((\ev_{1},\ev_{2}),\iota_{f,0}\times\iota_{f,\infty})}(D_{f,0}\times D_{f,\infty})

where
\beta
runs over the set of elements of
H_{2}(\mathcal{E}_{f}(G/P))
satisfying
(\pi_{f})_{*}\beta=[\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\pt]
and
\langle c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{\rho}),\beta\rangle=\rho(\eta)
for any
\rho\in(Q^{\vee}/Q^{\vee}_{P})^{*}
.

(2)

Define

\ev_{f,\eta,0}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{0,\infty}(f,\eta)\rightarrow D_{f,0}

to be the morphism induced by
\ev_{1}
.

(3)

Define

\ev_{f,\eta,\infty}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{0,\infty}(f,\eta)\rightarrow D_{f,\infty}\simeq\Gamma\times G/P\rightarrow G/P

to be the composition of the morphism induced by
\ev_{2}
, the isomorphism induced by
\nu_{f}
and the projection.

Remark 5.2
.

The superscripts
0,\infty
in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{0,\infty}(f,\eta)
refer to the marked points
0,\infty\in\mathbb{P}^{1}
while the subscripts
0,2
in
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,2}(\mathcal{E}_{f}(G/P),\beta)
refer to the genus (
=0
) and the number of marked points (
=2
) as usual.

Let
H
be either
T
or
G
. Put
\widehat{H}:=H\times\mathbb{G}_{m}
. Let
f:\Gamma\rightarrow\mathcal{G}r
be a morphism. Suppose
\Gamma
has an
\widehat{H}
-action and
f
is
\widehat{H}
-equivariant. By modifying the proof of
[
9
, Lemma 3.5]
, we see that
\mathcal{E}_{f}(G/P)
has an
\widehat{H}
-action such that
\pi_{f}
is
\widehat{H}
-equivariant,
D_{f,0}
and
D_{f,\infty}
are
\widehat{H}
-invariant and the isomorphism
D_{f,\infty}\simeq\Gamma\times G/P
induced by
\nu_{f}
is
\widehat{H}
-equivariant. (Here,
H
acts trivially on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
and
\mathbb{G}_{m}
acts on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
by rotation such that
0\in\mathbb{P}^{1}
has weight 1.) It follows that the stack
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{0,\infty}(f,\eta)
has a natural
\widehat{H}
-action and the morphisms
\ev_{f,\eta,0}
and
\ev_{f,\eta,\infty}
are
\widehat{H}
-equivariant.

We will use the stacks
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{0,\infty}(f,\eta)
to define the non-abelian shift operators. Before doing so, we need to define the following convolution-type operation. As before, let
H
be either
T
or
G
and
f:\Gamma\rightarrow\mathcal{G}r
an
\widehat{H}
-equivariant morphism.

Definition 5.3
.

(C.f.
[
17
, end of Section 3.5]
)
Define an
H_{\widehat{H}}^{\bullet}(\pt)
-linear map

m_{f}:H^{\widehat{H}}_{-\diamond}(\Gamma)\otimes_{H_{\widehat{G}}^{\bullet}(\pt)}H_{\widehat{G}}^{*}(G/P)\rightarrow H_{\widehat{H}}^{\dim_{\mathbb{R}}\Gamma+\diamond+*}(D_{f,0})

to be the composition

\displaystyle m_{f}:~

\displaystyle H^{\widehat{H}}_{-\diamond}(\Gamma)\otimes_{H_{\widehat{G}}^{\bullet}(\pt)}H_{\widehat{G}}^{*}(G/P)\xrightarrow[(a)]{\sim}H_{H\times\widehat{G}}^{\dim_{\mathbb{R}}\Gamma+\diamond}(\mathcal{E}_{f,0})\otimes_{H_{\widehat{G}}^{\bullet}(\pt)}H_{\widehat{G}}^{*}(G/P)\xrightarrow[(b)]{}

\displaystyle H_{H\times(G\times\mathbb{G}_{m})}^{\dim_{\mathbb{R}}\Gamma+\diamond+*}(\mathcal{E}_{f,0}\times G/P)\xrightarrow[(c)]{\sim}H_{\widehat{H}}^{\dim_{\mathbb{R}}\Gamma+\diamond+*}(\mathcal{E}_{f,0}\times^{G}G/P)\xrightarrow[(d)]{\sim}H^{\dim_{\mathbb{R}}\Gamma+\diamond+*}_{\widehat{H}}(D_{f,0})

where
\mathcal{E}_{f,0}:=\mathcal{E}_{f}|_{0\times\Gamma}
and the above morphisms are induced by

(a)

the sequence of canonical isomorphisms

H^{\widehat{H}}_{-\diamond}(\Gamma)\simeq H_{\widehat{H}}^{\dim_{\mathbb{R}}\Gamma+\diamond}(\Gamma)\simeq H_{\widehat{H}}^{\dim_{\mathbb{R}}\Gamma+\diamond}(\mathcal{E}_{f,0}/G)\simeq H_{\widehat{H}\times G}^{\dim_{\mathbb{R}}\Gamma+\diamond}(\mathcal{E}_{f,0})\simeq H_{H\times\widehat{G}}^{\dim_{\mathbb{R}}\Gamma+\diamond}(\mathcal{E}_{f,0});

(b)

the external cup product (
G\times\mathbb{G}_{m}
acts on
\mathcal{E}_{f,0}\times G/P
diagonally);

(c)

the free group action on
\mathcal{E}_{f,0}\times G/P
by the diagonal subgroup of
G\times G
; and

(d)

the canonical isomorphism
\mathcal{E}_{f,0}\times^{G}G/P\simeq D_{f,0}
.

Recall the opposite Schubert basis
\{\sigma^{v}\}_{v\in W^{P}}
of
H_{\widehat{T}}^{\bullet}(G/P)
defined in Section
3.1
.

Definition 5.4
.

Let
f:\Gamma\rightarrow\mathcal{G}r
be a
\widehat{T}
-equivariant morphism. For any
x\in H_{-\bullet}^{\widehat{T}}(\Gamma)
,
y\in H_{\widehat{G}}^{*}(G/P)
,
v\in W^{P}
and
\eta\in Q^{\vee}/Q^{\vee}_{P}
, define

\mathcal{I}(f,x,y,v,\eta):=\int_{[\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{0,\infty}(f,\eta)]^{vir}}\ev_{f,\eta,0}^{*}m_{f}(x\otimes y)\cup\ev_{f,\eta,\infty}^{*}\sigma^{v}\in H^{\diamond}_{\widehat{T}}(\pt)

where
\diamond:=\bullet+*-2\ell(v)-2\sum_{\alpha\in R^{+}\setminus R^{+}_{P}}\alpha(\eta)
.

5.2.
Definition

Recall the affine Schubert basis
\{\xi_{wt_{\lambda}}\}_{wt_{\lambda}\in W_{af}^{-}}
and the Schubert basis
\{\sigma_{v}\}_{v\in W^{P}}
defined in Section
2.1
and Section
3.1
respectively.

Definition 5.5
.

Define an
H_{\widehat{T}}^{\bullet}(\pt)[q_{i}^{\pm 1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]
-linear map

-\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{GMP}^{T}-:H_{-\diamond}^{\widehat{T}}(\mathcal{G}r)\otimes_{H_{\widehat{G}}^{\bullet}(\pt)}QH_{\widehat{G}}^{*}(G/P)[q_{i}^{-1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]\rightarrow QH_{\widehat{T}}^{\diamond+*}(G/P)[q_{i}^{-1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]

by

(5.1)

\xi_{wt_{\lambda}}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{GMP}^{T}y:=\sum_{v\in W^{P}}\sum_{\eta\in Q^{\vee}/Q^{\vee}_{P}}q^{\eta}\mathcal{I}(f_{\mathcal{G}r,wt_{\lambda}},[\Gamma_{wt_{\lambda}}],y,v,\eta)\sigma_{v},

where
\mathcal{I}(f_{\mathcal{G}r,wt_{\lambda}},[\Gamma_{wt_{\lambda}}],y,v,\eta)
is the integral defined in Definition
5.4
with
f
taken to be a
\widehat{T}
-equivariant morphism
f_{\mathcal{G}r,wt_{\lambda}}:\Gamma_{wt_{\lambda}}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}r
which factors through a resolution
\Gamma_{wt_{\lambda}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{B}\cdot t^{w(\lambda)}}
.

Lemma 5.6
.

-\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{GMP}^{T}-
is graded.

Proof.

By Definition
5.4
, the integral
\mathcal{I}(f_{\mathcal{G}r,wt_{\lambda}},[\Gamma_{wt_{\lambda}}],y,v,\eta)
from (
5.1
) has degree
-2\ell(wt_{\lambda})+\deg y-2\ell(v)-2\sum_{\alpha\in R^{+}\setminus R^{+}_{P}}\alpha(\eta)
. Since
q^{\eta}
and
\sigma_{v}
has degree
2\sum_{\alpha\in R^{+}\setminus R^{+}_{P}}\alpha(\eta)
and
2\ell(v)
respectively,
\xi_{wt_{\lambda}}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{GMP}^{T}y
has degree
-2\ell(wt_{\lambda})+\deg y
which is the degree of
\xi_{wt_{\lambda}}\otimes y
in
H_{-\bullet}^{\widehat{T}}(\mathcal{G}r)\otimes_{H_{\widehat{G}}^{\bullet}(\pt)}QH_{\widehat{G}}^{\bullet}(G/P)[q_{i}^{-1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]
.
∎

Let
f:\Gamma\rightarrow\mathcal{G}r
be a
\widehat{T}
-equivariant morphism. By a localization argument (see e.g. the proof of
[
9
, Proposition 3.12]
), we have

(5.2)

f_{*}x\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{GMP}^{T}y=\sum_{v\in W^{P}}\sum_{\eta\in Q^{\vee}/Q^{\vee}_{P}}q^{\eta}\mathcal{I}(f,x,y,v,\eta)\sigma_{v}

for any
x\in H_{-\diamond}^{\widehat{T}}(\Gamma)
and
y\in QH_{\widehat{G}}^{*}(G/P)[q_{i}^{-1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]
. Suppose further
f
is
\widehat{G}
-equivariant. Then for any
\eta\in Q^{\vee}/Q^{\vee}_{P}
, the stack
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{0,\infty}(f,\eta)
has a natural
\widehat{G}
-action such that
\ev_{f,\eta,0}
and
\ev_{f,\eta,\infty}
are
\widehat{G}
-equivariant. It follows that, by (
5.2
), we have

f_{*}(w\cdot x)\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{GMP}^{T}y=w\cdot(f_{*}x\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{GMP}^{T}y)

for any
w\in W
. Since every
\mathcal{G}r^{\leqslant\lambda}
has a
\widehat{G}
-equivariant resolution, we have proved

Lemma 5.7
.

-\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{GMP}^{T}-|_{H_{-\diamond}^{\widehat{G}}(\mathcal{G}r)\otimes_{H_{\widehat{G}}^{\bullet}(\pt)}QH_{\widehat{G}}^{*}(G/P)[q_{i}^{-1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]}
lands in
QH_{\widehat{G}}^{\diamond+*}(G/P)[q_{i}^{-1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]
.
\square

Definition 5.8
.

(Non-abelian shift operators) Define

-\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{GMP}^{G}-:H_{-\diamond}^{\widehat{G}}(\mathcal{G}r)\otimes_{H_{\widehat{G}}^{\bullet}(\pt)}QH_{\widehat{G}}^{*}(G/P)[q_{i}^{-1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]\rightarrow QH_{\widehat{G}}^{\diamond+*}(G/P)[q_{i}^{-1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]

to be the map induced by
-\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{GMP}^{T}-
via Lemma
5.7
.

5.3.
Some properties

Let
H
be either
T
or
G
.

Proposition 5.9
.

For any
x_{1}\in H^{\widehat{H}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{G}r)
,
x_{2}\in H^{\widehat{G}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{G}r)
and
y\in QH^{\bullet}_{\widehat{G}}(G/P)[q_{i}^{-1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]
, we have

x_{1}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{GMP}^{H}(x_{2}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{GMP}^{G}y)=(x_{1}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{\mathcal{G}r}^{H}x_{2})\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{GMP}^{H}y

where
-\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{\mathcal{G}r}^{H}-
is defined in Section
2.1
.

Proof.

This is the algebraic version of
[
17
, Theorem 4.3]
. Our proof is nothing but a translation of the proof given therein.

By localization, it suffices to assume
H=T
and
x_{1}=[t^{\mu_{0}}]
for some
\mu_{0}\in Q^{\vee}
. There exist
p_{0},p_{1},\ldots,p_{N}\in H_{\widehat{T}}^{\bullet}(\pt)
with
p_{0}\neq 0
and
\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{N}\in Q^{\vee}
such that

p_{0}x_{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{N}p_{j}[t^{\mu_{j}}].

For
j=0,\ldots,N
, put
p_{j}^{\prime}:=\twist_{-\mu_{0}}(p_{j})
where
\twist_{-\mu_{0}}
is defined in (
3.1
). Using the fact that
\mathbb{S}^{A}_{\mu_{0}}
is
(-\mu_{0})
-twisted
H_{\widehat{T}}^{\bullet}(\pt)
-linear (Lemma
3.4
), we have

\displaystyle p^{\prime}_{0}x_{1}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{GMP}^{T}(x_{2}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{GMP}^{G}y)

\displaystyle=p^{\prime}_{0}\mathbb{S}^{A}_{\mu_{0}}(x_{2}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{GMP}^{G}y)=\mathbb{S}^{A}_{\mu_{0}}(p_{0}x_{2}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{GMP}^{G}y)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{N}\mathbb{S}^{A}_{\mu_{0}}(p_{j}\mathbb{S}^{A}_{\mu_{j}}(y))=\sum_{j=1}^{N}p^{\prime}_{j}\mathbb{S}^{A}_{\mu_{0}}\circ\mathbb{S}^{A}_{\mu_{j}}(y).

By Lemma
3.6
, we have
\mathbb{S}^{A}_{\mu_{0}}\circ\mathbb{S}^{A}_{\mu_{j}}=\mathbb{S}^{A}_{\mu_{0}+\mu_{j}}
, and hence

p_{0}^{\prime}x_{1}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{GMP}^{T}(x_{2}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{GMP}^{G}y)=\sum_{j=1}^{N}p^{\prime}_{j}\mathbb{S}^{A}_{\mu_{0}+\mu_{j}}(y)=\left(\sum_{j=1}^{N}p^{\prime}_{j}[t^{\mu_{0}+\mu_{j}}]\right)\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{GMP}^{G}y.

By the definition of
-\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{\mathcal{G}r}^{T}-
(see Section
2.1
), we have
\sum_{j=1}^{N}p_{j}^{\prime}[t^{\mu_{0}+\mu_{j}}]=p_{0}^{\prime}x_{1}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{\mathcal{G}r}^{T}x_{2}
, and hence

p^{\prime}_{0}x_{1}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{GMP}^{T}(x_{2}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{GMP}^{G}y)=p^{\prime}_{0}(x_{1}\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{\mathcal{G}r}^{T}x_{2})\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{GMP}^{G}y.

The result follows.
∎

Proposition 5.10
.

For any
x\in H^{\widehat{H}}_{\bullet}(\mathcal{G}r)
,
y\in QH_{\widehat{G}}^{\bullet}(G/P)[q_{i}^{-1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]
and
i\in I\setminus I_{P}
, we have

(5.3)

x\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{GMP}^{H}(\nabla^{G}_{\partial_{q_{i}}}y)=\nabla^{H}_{\partial_{q_{i}}}(x\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{GMP}^{H}y).

Proof.

This is the algebraic version of
[
17
, Theorem 4.5]
. Our proof is nothing but a translation of the proof given therein.

By localization, it suffices to assume
H=T
and
x=[t^{\mu}]
for some
\mu\in Q^{\vee}
. In this case, the LHS of (
5.3
) is equal to
\mathbb{S}^{A}_{\mu}(\nabla^{T}_{\partial_{q_{i}}}y)
. By Lemma
3.7
, the latter expression is equal to
\nabla^{T}_{\partial_{q_{i}}}\mathbb{S}^{A}_{\mu}(y)
which is equal to the RHS of (
5.3
).
∎

5.4.
Some computational results

Definition 5.11
.

Let
H
be either
T
or
G
. Define an
H_{\widehat{H}}^{\bullet}(\pt)
-linear map

\Phi_{GMP}^{H}:H^{\widehat{H}}_{-\bullet}(\mathcal{G}r)\rightarrow QH_{\widehat{H}}^{\bullet}(G/P)[q_{i}^{-1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]

by

\Phi_{GMP}^{H}(x):=x\mathchoice{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\displaystyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\textstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.5}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bullet$}}}}}_{GMP}^{H}1,\quad x\in H_{-\bullet}^{\widehat{H}}(\mathcal{G}r).

It is graded by Lemma
5.6
.

Observe that
m_{f}([\Gamma]\otimes 1)=1
for any
\widehat{T}
-equivariant morphism
f:\Gamma\rightarrow\mathcal{G}r
. Hence

\mathcal{I}(f,[\Gamma],1,v,\eta)=\int_{[\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{0,\infty}(f,\eta)]^{vir}}\ev_{f,\eta,\infty}^{*}\sigma^{v}=\int_{[\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\infty}(f,\eta)]^{vir}}\ev_{f,\eta,\infty}^{*}\sigma^{v}

where
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\infty}(f,\eta):=\bigcup_{\beta}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,1}(\mathcal{E}_{f}(G/P),\beta)\times_{(\ev_{1},\iota_{f,\infty})}D_{f,\infty}
. It follows that
\Phi_{GMP}^{T}
has the same defining expression (i.e. the expression (
5.1
) after putting
y=1
) as that of
Savelyev-Seidel’s homomorphism

\Phi_{SS}
defined in
[
9
, Definition 3.10]
, except that the latter is in the
T
-equivariant settings. This observation allows us to obtain some computational results for
\Phi_{GMP}^{T}
by extending those for
\Phi_{SS}
which have already been established in
[
9
]
and
[
10
]
.

Denote by
(W^{P})_{af}
the set of
wt_{\lambda}\in W_{af}
satisfying

\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}\alpha\in R_{P}^{+}\cap(-w^{-1}R^{+})&\Longrightarrow&\alpha(\lambda)=-1\\[5.0pt]
\alpha\in R_{P}^{+}\cap w^{-1}R^{+}&\Longrightarrow&\alpha(\lambda)=0\end{array}\right..

Proposition 5.12
.

For any
wt_{\lambda}\in W_{af}^{-}
, we have

\Phi_{GMP}^{T}(\xi_{wt_{\lambda}})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}q^{[\lambda]}\sigma_{\widetilde{w}}&wt_{\lambda}\in(W^{P})_{af}\\[5.0pt]
0&\text{otherwise}\end{array}\right.

where
\widetilde{w}\in W^{P}
is the unique element satisfying
\widetilde{w}W_{P}=wW_{P}
.

Proof.

This is the
\widehat{T}
-equivariant version of
[
9
, Theorem 4.9]
. Recall the proof given therein consists of the following steps:

(1)

Show that each
f_{\mathcal{G}r,wt_{\lambda}}:\Gamma_{wt_{\lambda}}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}r
(see Definition
5.5
) can be chosen to be equivariant with respect to the
B_{-}
-action and the
U_{\alpha,k}
-action for any
\alpha\in R
and
k>0

[
9
, Lemma 2.4]
, where
U_{\alpha,k}:=\exp(z^{k}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha})\subset\mathcal{L}G
is the affine root group.

(2)

Show that the stack
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\infty}(f_{\mathcal{G}r,wt_{\lambda}},\eta)
(denoted by
\overline{\mathcal{M}}(f_{\mathcal{G}r,wt_{\lambda}},\eta)
in
op. cit.
) is smooth and of expected dimension
[
9
, Proposition 4.5]
, and the evaluation morphism

\ev_{f_{\mathcal{G}r,wt_{\lambda}},\eta,\infty}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\infty}(f_{\mathcal{G}r,wt_{\lambda}},\eta)\rightarrow G/P

(denoted by
\ev
in
op. cit.
) is transverse to any
B
-equivariant morphisms to
G/P

[
9
, before Lemma 4.7]
.

(3)

Compute the
T
-equivariant analogue of the integral
\mathcal{I}(f_{\mathcal{G}r,wt_{\lambda}},[\Gamma_{wt_{\lambda}}],1,v,\eta)
by counting the elements of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{\infty}(f_{\mathcal{G}r,wt_{\lambda}},\eta)\times_{(\ev_{f_{\mathcal{G}r,wt_{\lambda}},\eta,\infty},f_{G/P,v})}\Gamma_{v}
directly
[
9
, Proposition 4.8]
, where
f_{G/P,v}:\Gamma_{v}\rightarrow G/P
is a
B
-equivariant morphism which factors through a resolution of
\overline{B\dot{v}P/P}
.

It is not difficult to see that the arguments in
op. cit.
for completing the above steps work in the current settings. The only extra task is to show that the chosen
f_{\mathcal{G}r,wt_{\lambda}}
is also
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant. But this is automatic because it satisfies the property that every algebraic group action on
\overline{\mathcal{B}\cdot t^{w(\lambda)}}
lifts to an algebraic group action on
\Gamma_{wt_{\lambda}}
.
∎

Corollary 5.13
.

The map

(\Phi_{GMP}^{T})_{\mathbb{C}[q_{i}^{\pm 1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]}:H^{\widehat{T}}_{-\bullet}(\mathcal{G}r)[q_{i}^{\pm 1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]\rightarrow QH_{\widehat{T}}^{\bullet}(G/P)[q_{i}^{-1}|~i\in I\setminus I_{P}]

defined by extension of scalars is surjective.
\square

Proposition 5.14
.

For any
\lambda\in Q^{\vee}_{+}
,
\Phi_{GMP}^{T}
vanishes on
H_{>2\rho(\lambda-w_{P}w_{0}(\lambda))}^{\widehat{T}}(\mathcal{G}r^{\leqslant\lambda})
.

Proof.

The proof is identical to the proof of
[
10
, Proposition 3.9]
.
∎

Proposition 5.15
.

For any
\lambda\in Q^{\vee}_{+}
and
x\in H_{2\rho(\lambda-w_{P}w_{0}(\lambda))}^{\widehat{T}}(\mathcal{G}r^{\leqslant\lambda})
, we have
\Phi_{GMP}^{T}(x)=c_{x}q^{[w_{0}(\lambda)]}
for some
c_{x}\in\mathbb{C}
.

Proof.

This follows from the proof of
[
10
, Proposition 3.11]
.
∎

6.
Bezrukavnikov-Finkelberg’s theorems

In this section, we give a brief exposition of two results of Bezrukavnikov and Finkelberg
[
6
]
. Recall the geometric Satake equivalence
[
14
,
39
]
is an equivalence

\gs:\rep(G^{\vee})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{P}_{G(\mathcal{O})}(\mathcal{G}r)

of tensor categories between the category
\rep(G^{\vee})
of finite dimensional representations of
G^{\vee}
and the category
\mathcal{P}_{G(\mathcal{O})}(\mathcal{G}r)
of
G(\mathcal{O})
-equivariant perverse sheaves on
\mathcal{G}r
with compact support. The first of their results, discussed in Section
6.1
